# awesome-noodles 🍜

Forty-three workflows for [nanoodle](https://nanoodle.com): models pass images, stories, motion and sound to one another to make something you can play, watch or remix. Open one, change its inputs, and run with your NanoGPT key and balance. Viewing the [saved results](https://nanoodle.com/examples/gallery/) is free; opening a workflow does not run it.

| Continue a story |
| --- |
| [![Storyboard relay: a postal robot](evidence/storyboard-relay/orbital-delivery-2026-09-12T14-01-14-715Z/repair-image.webp)](https://nanoodle.com/examples/gallery/#storyboard-relay) |
| Generate → continue → critique → repair → inspect again. [Compare the frames](evidence/README.md#storyboard-relay). |

Storyboard relay also has a second saved run: a cut-paper crab flying a kite. [Compare both versions](evidence/README.md), or open its gallery result to remix the exact inputs. Reviews retain the remaining continuity and material mistakes.

- **Idea to 3D model** — GLM writes a concept description, Muse paints one clean concept render, and TRELLIS.2 turns it into a textured GLB you can orbit (~$0.21). - **Photo to scene mesh** — One photo of a real place becomes a textured 3D mesh of it: Gemini surveys the scene near to far, MoGe 3 reconstructs the visible geometry into a GLB you can orbit, and GLM writes the viewing guide, honest edges included (~$0.03–0.05). - **One photo, whole campaign** — One product photo becomes a finished sale poster in three chained Seedream 5.5 edits — backdrop, props, offer text — while a Gemini anchor read and a blind re-read feed a GLM drift report that measures the no-drift claim instead of trusting it (~$0.09). - **Edit with references** — One main photo plus up to three reference pictures and one instruction that names each picture: MiniMax H3-Image fuses them into a single scene (the jacket from Picture 2 on the person in Picture 1, the sunglasses from Picture 3, the puppy from Picture 4), and a survey → blind re-read → checker chain grades whether every piece landed and the subject survived (~$0.07). - **Photo to 3D splat** — One photo of one object becomes a 3D Gaussian splat you can spin: Gemini surveys the object and the sides the photo hides, Idol (Sperid Labs) rebuilds it as hundreds of thousands of coloured points — hidden sides invented and named as invented — and GLM writes the guide to the splat and its honest edges (~$0.10). [Open in - **Humanize a comment** — Five writers (Qwen3.8 Novelist, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, Gemma-4-12B Starry Ocean, Qwen3.8 antislop and Mercury 3), each with its own voice, rewrite a stiff draft reply in Nano Maxi's voice (flow v2.7); a GLM-5.3 decider checks each rewrite for faithfulness (who said what, no new facts), ranks them and picks the most human faithful one, or keeps the original. Returns one JSON object {winner, text, why, ranked}, where ranked lists all five writers with faithful, disqualified and reason (on mcp.nanoodle.com also each writer's model and exact text; a standalone graph run returns writer letters only, plus the winner's text) (typical run ~$0.003 before the 20% markup, ~$0.0036 after; hosted deposit pinned at $0.05428204, about 0.163 XNO plus a small matching tag at $0.333/XNO; the XNO amount moves with the rate). Draft and Context are required, and Guidance is optional (default "a friendly, specific reply"). The writer and decider prompts and the block labels are locked, so they aren't tool args. Each writer has a 60 s timeout and is skipped on failure, so the decider still runs on the survivors and returns ORIGINAL if all five fail. This needs a nanoodle engine with per-node timeout, skip and lock support (nanoodle-js #46). - **ADetailer finisher** — The ComfyUI ADetailer pattern rebuilt from Nanoodle parts: a vision model lists face and hand defects, an LLM turns them into a surgical edit instruction, two edit models take refine passes, a Decide pick keeps the most faithful of the original and both passes, and the winner is upscaled. - **Fridge to feast** — A vision model inventories your fridge photo, a chef LLM invents a no-shopping recipe from only what is there, Muse renders the plate, and a Decide judge audits the render against the recipe — faithful plate, minor liberties, phantom ingredient or wrong dish (~$0.02). [Open in - **Pet portrait, likeness-checked** — A vision model writes your pet's identity card — the numbered markings that make the animal recognisably itself — an LLM art historian picks a historical painting style to suit its temperament, Muse Edit paints the portrait from the photo, and a second vision read-back plus a likeness auditor score the painting mark by mark: kept, changed, lost, with a Decide verdict on top (~$0.04). - **Menu to plate, checked** — A vision model transcribes and translates a photographed foreign menu into a numbered menu card, an LLM diner picks one dish against your brief and writes an order slip with the exact components the menu promises (plus a Not-promised line), Muse renders the plate from text alone, and a second vision read plus an auditor score the render component by component. - **Haircut preview, identity-checked** — A vision model writes your face card — the numbered anchors that make you recognisably you, with your current hair on its own line as the one thing allowed to change — an LLM stylist picks one cut that suits your face and your brief, Muse Edit renders it on the original photo, and a second vision read-back plus an identity auditor score the preview anchor by anchor: kept, changed, lost, with a Decide verdict on top (~$0.04). - **Bookshelf next-read, phantom-checked** — A vision model reads your shelf spine by spine into a numbered inventory, an LLM librarian picks ONE next read for your brief citing only that inventory number, Muse renders the pick as a reading still life with its title and author on the cover, and a two-pass auditor checks both cheats — a librarian inventing a book, and a renderer garbling the cover lettering — with a Decide verdict on top (~$0.03). - **Bar chart, number-checked** — A GLM data clerk writes a 5-line dataset (five cafe drinks, each with an exact number of cups sold and its own bar colour), a second GLM art-directs it, Muse renders a clean bar chart poster, a vision checker reads the printed numbers back off the bars, and an auditor scores charted / wrong number / wrong order / missing, with a Decide verdict on top. - **Metro map, station-checked** — A GLM transit planner writes a two-line network spec (Line A west to east and Line B north to south, five stations each, one shared interchange as stop 3 on both lines), a second GLM art-directs it, Muse renders a clean schematic metro map, a vision checker traces each line and reads the station labels back in order, and an auditor scores mapped / wrong order / wrong line / missing plus a separate interchange check, with a Decide verdict on top. - **Clean-voice rescue, transcript-scored** — Seedance films a baker shouting one exact line in a loud farmers market, Whisper transcribes the noisy take, ElevenLabs Audio Isolation strips everything but her voice, and Whisper transcribes again — a GLM auditor scores what the cleaning recovered, word by word. - **Clip to comic, order-checked** — Sample 6 frames from an uploaded clip, have a Gemini clerk log its 4 beats in chronological order with a short caption each, and let Muse paint them as a 2 by 2 comic page. A second vision read inventories the page panel by panel, a GLM auditor scores each event IN ORDER, SWAPPED or MISSING with its caption KEPT, GARBLED or LOST, and a Decide judge labels it story in order, panels shuffled, events missing or different story. - **Score the still, ear-checked** — A vision model reads a Muse still into a SOUND CARD (mood, energy, pace, leading instrument family, arc — a closed vocabulary fixed before any music exists), a GLM composer turns the card into an instrumental prompt, ElevenLabs Music composes a 20-second cue, and a Qwen omni model listens to the cue blind through the LLM node's audio port and reports back in the same five axes for an axis-by-axis audit (~$0.24). - **Words only, repainted** — A vision model inventories your photo into 8 numbered facts in fixed slots, a GLM writer turns them into one prompt, and Muse repaints the scene from those words alone — the painter never sees the photo. A second vision read inventories the repaint in the same slots, a GLM auditor scores SURVIVED / CHANGED / LOST slot by slot with INVENTED lines for anything the words never said, and a Decide judge labels it faithful repaint, mostly survived, half lost or different scene. - **Palette transplant, re-measured** — A vision model measures a Muse source scene into a PALETTE CARD (6 swatches: colour name, approximate hex, share of the picture, plus a light line), a GLM director assigns every swatch to elements of a completely unrelated target scene and bans its natural colours, Muse paints that scene from the card alone — the painter never sees the source picture — and the same clerk re-measures the new picture blind so a GLM auditor can score swatch by swatch which colours actually crossed (~$0.03). - **Hear it, paint it** — ElevenLabs SFX performs a four-event sound script as a 24-second soundscape, a Qwen omni model listens blind through the LLM node's audio port and cards the events it actually heard in order, a GLM painter turns that card alone into a scene prompt, Muse paints it, and a two-hop audit scores script vs hearing and hearing vs painting event by event (~$0.10). - **Spot the difference, diff-checked** — Muse paints a cluttered wizard's desk, a Gemini clerk cards its objects, a GLM planner picks five card items and plans one big change each (a remove, a recolour, or an add beside an anchor), a second GLM compresses the plan into one surgical edit instruction, Muse Edit applies it, and a Gemini spotter shown BOTH pictures at once — and never the plan — lists every difference it can see, so a GLM auditor can score each change SPOTTED or NOT SPOTTED and name any unplanned drift (~$0.04). - **Hidden word, object-coded** — A GLM coder hides your word in a row of shelf objects, one per letter, each picked so its most common name starts with that letter; a second GLM turns the plan into a still-life prompt that never mentions the word, Muse paints the shelf (no text anywhere in the picture — the message is in the choice of objects), a blind Gemini reader names the objects left to right, and a GLM decoder spells what those names actually say, scoring each letter KEPT, RENAMED (right object, synonym, letter dead), WRONG OBJECT or MISSING (~$0.03). - **Maze run, solve-checked** — A Gemini architect designs a formal 7×7 block maze as text (a coordinate route, a grid built from it, and its own solution), a second GLM turns the grid into a row-by-row painting brief that never mentions the solution, Muse paints it, a blind Gemini solver transcribes the render back into a character grid and proposes a path, and a GLM auditor simulates every walk on both grids — the plan's solution on the plan, the solver's path on both grids — so a lost route is charged to the painter, the reader or the solver. - **Mirror check, reflection-audited** — A GLM director turns a shore brief into a prompt for one glassy lakeshore, Muse paints the scene and its reflection in a single render, and the same picture is read twice by two blind Gemini readers — one inventories only the landmarks above the waterline, the other only the reflections in the water — so a GLM mirror auditor can line the two cards up position by position (MIRRORED, COLOUR SHIFTED, WRONG OBJECT, MISSING REFLECTION, plus extras and order) and a Decide judge can call it a true mirror or catch the water inventing (~$0.03). [Open in - **Shadow check, sun-audited** — A GLM director writes a SUN CARD (the sun on one side at one elevation, which fixes by a stated factor how long every shadow must be relative to its caster's height, plus five objects with heights in units on one flat ground line), a second GLM turns the card into a flat side-elevation painting brief, Muse paints it, a blind Gemini surveyor measures the rendered picture — sun side, and each object's shadow side and shadow length as a ratio category against that object's own height — and a GLM auditor scores the measurements against the card's computed expectation: direction X of 5, lengths MATCH / NEAR / OFF, orphan shadows. - **Squint test, two-distance-checked** — A GLM director writes a SCALE CARD (one big FAR SHAPE, one small MATERIAL in a dark SHAPE COLOUR and a pale FILL COLOUR), a second GLM turns it into a mosaic flat-lay prompt, and Muse paints a dense top-down carpet of the objects with the dark ones forming the shape. The same picture is then read twice on physically different pixels: a blind Gemini close reader inventories the objects at full resolution, while a second blind Gemini far reader gets the picture after a local Resize node shrinks it to 64 pixels wide and names the big shape that survives the squint. A GLM auditor scores FAR match, CLOSE material and colours X of 3, and SEPARATION — clean if the far reader could no longer make out individual objects, leaked if it could. - **Stack check, support-audited** — A GLM director writes a STACK CARD (exactly 6 stackable objects bottom to top, each with a colour, a width category that never increases upward, and a RESTS ON line naming the object directly below it), a second GLM turns it into a flat side-elevation painter prompt, and Muse paints the tower. A blind Gemini surveyor inventories the rendered tower's contacts — for each object, what it actually rests on and whether the contact is full, partial or none — and a GLM auditor scores IDENTITY and SUPPORT X of 6 against the card and computes a transitive GROUNDED closure over the survey alone (one break ungrounds every level above it), naming the FIRST BREAK and any FLOATING levels. - **Turnaround check, viewpoint-audited** — A GLM director writes a FRONT CARD (exactly 5 display pieces chosen from a menu with canonical front and back features, a distinct colour each, in front-view order), a second GLM turns it into a front-view painter prompt, and Muse paints the shelf from the front. A GLM predictor then performs the viewpoint change on paper — back position 1 is front position 5, the order fully reversed, each piece's BACK features moved to SHOWS and its FRONT features to HIDES — a third GLM turns that prediction into a back-view prompt, and Muse generates the back view from text alone, sharing no pixels with the front. A blind Gemini surveyor inventories the back view, and a GLM auditor scores IDENTITY, position-by-position ORDER against the reversal, BACKS SHOWN (X of 15), and FRONT LEAK — the front features (eyes, faces, headlights) a flat-picture generator paints anyway when asked for a back. [Open in - **Scale check, proportion-audited** — A GLM director writes a SCALE CARD (exactly 6 objects chosen from a menu with canonical real-world heights in centimetres, pairwise at least 20 cm apart, tallest to shortest), a second GLM turns it into a flat side-elevation painter prompt with each height stated as a comparison against the tallest, and Muse paints the line-up on one ground line. A blind Gemini surveyor measures each rendered object's height as a percentage of the tallest object's picture height, and a GLM auditor computes each object's expected share from the card's centimetres and scores IDENTITY, in-band SCALE (±15 points) and height ORDER, and reads the signed errors for mean regression — the known generator failure where small objects swell and large ones shrink toward a typical size. - **Stereo check, disparity-audited** — A GLM director writes a DEPTH CARD (exactly 5 diorama pieces from a menu, a distinct colour, slot and depth rank each, planned shifts 8/6/4/2/1 percent of panel width by rank, plus the painted backdrop sun as a zero-shift control), a second GLM turns it into a prompt for a side-by-side stereo pair, and Muse paints both panels in one generation — same diorama, same sizes, only horizontal positions displaced, near pieces shifting most. A blind Gemini surveyor measures each piece's centre in each panel, and a GLM auditor subtracts the pairs and scores IDENTITY, SHIFT accuracy (±3 points), DEPTH ORDER, the CONTROL, and FLAT — the flat-copy failure where two identical panels carry no depth at all. - **Negative space check, figure-ground-audited** — A GLM director writes a NEGATIVE CARD (one familiar SHAPE — star, heart, crescent, key, diamond, bell, arrow or bird — to be formed ONLY by the bright empty sky, the LEFT FORM and RIGHT FORM whose facing edges carve it, a FLOOR, and exactly 5 dark silhouette objects), a second GLM turns it into a prompt for a flat high-contrast silhouette scene, and Muse paints it — the shape itself is never drawn, only the dark forms around it. A blind Gemini surveyor lists the objects, then inverts figure and ground and names the shape the EMPTY central sky itself forms (or NONE), and a GLM auditor scores OBJECTS in position, SHAPE MATCH / NEAR / MISS, and GAP CLEAN / INTRUDED — the contamination axis for a region that should contain nothing at all. - **Perspective check, convergence-audited** — A GLM director writes a PERSPECTIVE CARD (one one-point scene from a menu, a deliberately off-centre vanishing point given as VP X / VP Y percentages — never 50 across — and exactly 5 side objects front to back), a second GLM turns it into a painter prompt stating the vanishing point in plain words, and Muse paints the scene. A blind Gemini surveyor extends the four receding edges, estimates where they actually meet, and judges each edge CONVERGES or PARALLEL, and a GLM auditor scores IDENTITY, VP ERROR on both axes (HIT within 8, NEAR within 15), CONVERGENCE X of 4, and FLAT — the flat-facade failure where receding lines stay parallel and the picture never recedes. - **Character to rigged, skin-verified** — A GLM concept writer expands a one-line character brief into a rig-ready reference (humanoid build, solid connected parts), a Join adds fixed T-pose reference rules, and Nano Banana paints the character. Tripo 2.5 lifts that single picture into a 3D statue — and then the statue's MESH travels the model wire into Hyper3D Rigging, a mesh-processing model that cannot run without an existing model, which fits a human skeleton and skin weights and returns a GLB that can be posed and animated. The proof is structural, not visual: the returned GLB is parsed for its skin (JOINTS_0 / WEIGHTS_0 attributes and a skins entry), because no screenshot can prove a model is rigged. - **Subject anywhere, preservation-audited** — A Gemini reader writes a PRESERVATION SPEC of an uploaded product photo (silhouette, colours, every printed word transcribed line by line), BRIA RMBG-3.0 cuts the subject out on transparency — a matting model, so the pixels are lifted, not repainted — and a Nano Banana edit builds a new scene around the cut-out under a do-not-alter instruction. A second, blind Gemini reader transcribes the placed subject and a GLM checker rules LABEL PRESERVED or DRIFTED line by line: the audit asks an identity question (is it still the same product?) instead of a beauty question, and microprint drift is a printed verdict, not a surprise. - **Relay drift, hop-checked** — A GLM director writes a SCENE CARD of exactly 6 numbered elements, Muse paints generation 1, and then only words travel: a blind Gemini reader inventories the picture and writes a recreation prompt, a GLM clerk extracts it verbatim, and Muse repaints generation 2 from those words alone — twice more, to generation 3. A GLM drift auditor scores how many card elements survive at EACH hop, which hop each loss happened at, and what the chain invented, and Decide headlines the trajectory (~$0.05). · [graph](graphs/relay-drift.noodle-graph.json) · [run headless](recipes/relay-drift.md)
- **Focus check, depth-of-field-audited** — A GLM director writes a FOCUS CARD (one depth-row scene from a menu, exactly 5 objects front to back at fixed graded distances — 1 / 3 / 6 / 10 / 15 metres — and a FOCAL object chosen from positions 2–4, never the nearest or farthest), a second GLM turns it into a shallow-depth-of-field painter prompt at f/1.8, and Muse paints the scene. A blind Gemini surveyor grades every object's SHARPNESS (SHARP / SLIGHTLY SOFT / SOFT / VERY BLURRED) and names the single sharpest position, and a GLM auditor scores IDENTITY, FOCUS PEAK (the sharpest object is the planned focal one), FALL-OFF X of 4 (sharpness strictly below the focal object's and never increasing with steps away from the focal plane on either side), and ALL SHARP — the deep-focus failure where nothing is blurred at all. Then the loop closes (the catalog's END IN THE ASSET exemplar): a GLM editor turns the audit into concrete edit notes, Muse Edit repaints the focus on the original picture, and a second blind surveyor plus the same auditor re-score the corrected picture against the same card — the flow's deliverable is the fixed image, not the verdict. - **Exploded check, disassembly-audited** — A GLM director writes an EXPLODED CARD (one object from a menu of separable things — cheeseburger, French press, camping lantern, desk lamp, coffee grinder — decomposed into exactly 6 canonical parts in disassembly order, top to bottom), a second GLM turns it into an exploded-view painter prompt (parts floating on one vertical axis, every part separated by a clear gap), and Muse paints it. A blind Gemini surveyor lists the parts it actually sees top to bottom and grades the gap below each (CLEAR GAP / TOUCHING / FUSED), and a GLM auditor scores PARTS X of 6, ORDER X of 5, SEPARATION X of 5 and STILL ASSEMBLED — the whole-object failure where every part is present but touching. - **Paint through music, round-trip** — Muse paints a scene from a brief, a Gemini sound scout cards the music it asks for on five closed axes (mood, energy, pace, leading family, arc), a GLM composer writes the cue prompt and ElevenLabs Music performs 20 seconds; then a Qwen omni listener hears the cue blind — no painting, no card — and reports the same axes, the instruments it heard, and a PICTURE line naming a scene the music could soundtrack. A GLM painter turns that report alone into a prompt and Muse paints the second painting, the deliverable: a scene that crossed into music and back, with a GLM round-trip audit and a Decide headline ('same scene heard back' / 'same mood, new scene' / 'mood lost' / 'silence or noise') kept as side outputs. Storyboard relay has [selected runs with actual outputs and review notes](evidence/README.md). Storyboard repairs improve the drafts while leaving visible continuity errors; the short films include audio assessed by a model. Follow the evidence links for those limitations and the [exploration report](docs/examples-rebuild-2026-09-12.md) for the design decisions.




## Run headless

Install either package:

```bash
npm install nanoodle
pip install nanoodle
```

From this checkout, inspect a graph without a key or API call:

```bash
npx nanoodle inspect graphs/bar-chart-audit.noodle-graph.json
# Python: nanoodle-py inspect graphs/bar-chart-audit.noodle-graph.json
```

To generate new output, set `NANOGPT_API_KEY` and follow a workflow's [recipe](recipes/). Runs spend your NanoGPT balance. Your key stays on your device; model requests go directly to the provider. The graph travels in the share link's URL fragment.

[Contribute a workflow](CONTRIBUTING.md) · [Curation decisions](CURATION.md) · [Run headless guide](https://nanoodle.com/guide/run-headless)
